ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u39	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I have the honour to present a report on the government draft act on organic farming and organic production, print No 2180. The need to develop the proposed rules results from the need to introduce into the Polish legal order the provisions of Regulation (EU) 2018/848 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 May 2018 on organic production and labelling of organic products. The provisions also aim to adapt the control system in organic farming to the requirements of Regulation (EU) 2017/625 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 March 2017 on official controls and other official activities. The project also pursues the objectives of the European Union in the area of food security and the development of organic production by introducing increased support for the use of certified and basic organic seed, increasing yield and quality, and consequently improving the availability of Polish organic products on the market. It also facilitates the use of fertilisers authorised for use in other European Union countries, thereby increasing the supply of fertilisers to organic producers. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission leaned on this project at two meetings, which took place on 26 April and 10 May this year. The discussion was actively attended by representatives of the social side. The Commission has taken into account some of their requests and some of them have rejected them. In conclusion, 27 amendments were tabled in the course of the work, of which 10 were accepted by the majority of committee members. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development welcomes the proposal presented by the government on organic farming and production and supports its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u40	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u41	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club vis-à-vis the government's draft law on organic farming and organic production. The need to adopt a new law in the Polish legal order is primarily due to two important amendments to EU law. The first concerns the revision of official controls in organic farming, while the second is due to the adoption of a new regulation on organic production and labelling of organic products. The draft law sets out in detail the powers of the authority in the organic production control and surveillance system and the functioning of a number of operators operating in the organic farming system, including certification bodies such as IJHARS, PIORiN, Central Centre for Plant Variety Research or KOWR. The solutions contained in the Act create fair conditions of market competition for all operators and protect consumers from unreliable and unreliable operators who, without applying organic production rules, would offer consumers products of poor commercial quality. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The proposed law introduces significant modifications to the current Organic Farming Act. It is impossible to discuss all the provisions contained in the contested bill. I will only refer to the very important ones that are introducing new solutions. The powers to carry out controls and certification of organic production have been transferred from the Minister responsible for Agriculture to the Chief Inspector of the IJHARS, who will also have the authority to issue guidelines and binding instructions to ensure the proper performance of the tasks carried out by the certification bodies. The draft law introduces provisions aimed at creating a single database of controlled and certified producers in organic farming, which will be available on the IJHARS website. The adoption of information on correctly completed reports of activities in organic farming will be carried out by the Regional Inspector IJHARS. The certification bodies will be required to publish the fees charged for carrying out checks and certification in organic farming. The draft law also makes it easier for fertilisers authorised in other countries of the European Union and Turkey. These fertilisers will be easier to import to Poland, which should increase their availability to organic farmers. The provisions of the Act also increase the financing of the adaptation of organic plant propagating material. It is intended to facilitate the introduction of a uniform application for consent for non-organic plant propagating material for farmers. Severe penalties have also been imposed for placing in advertising or commercial description of a product which does not comply with the organic farming rules, indications relating to organic production, which should improve the protection of consumers and producers against unfair practices. During the work of the Committee on the Law, there was a strong discussion about intensive action to support organic farming. The draft of this law, often called competence, mainly includes the implementation of EU law and cannot fully resolve issues related to the development of organic farming in Poland. In our country we have good conditions for the development of this agricultural field, but only 3.5% agricultural land is organically grown. The weakness of organic farming is high production costs, lack of developed distribution networks, fragmentation of supply and demand. Poles do not buy organic food because it is too expensive. Farmers often cannot sell their products, switch back to conventional production or find a foreign recipient. Green food is processed from the Polish raw material, where foreign producers make a lot of money. Some farms in Poland stop organic production with the end of subsidies, as without them production becomes insolvent. A thoughtful policy is therefore needed to support the production of the organic food market in Poland. Administrative barriers should also be removed, measures should be increased to promote organic food on the domestic market, which should increase customer awareness. Export opportunities must also be exploited, organic production must be combined with the development of agrotourism, organic products must be increased in collective nutrition, e.g. in schools or public institutions. The promotion of Polish organic food with the great potential of our agriculture can also translate into a positive image of Polish food products. Mrs. Marshal! The proposed provisions in the draft law provide for facilitation for organic producers and increase the availability of Polish organic products on the market, but there is still a legitimate need to work on supporting organic farming in Poland. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u42	Thank you very much, sir. The position of the Parliamentary Club The Civic Coalition will be presented by Małgorzata Tracz.

On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition - Civic Platform, Modern, Polish Initiative, Greens I present my position towards the government's draft law on organic farming and organic production, print No 2180. It's not a good project. This is a bad bill. It is a project that, instead of removing administrative and legal barriers in organic farming, these barriers will increase and hinder the development of organic farming for many years. It is a project which is opposed by environmental organisations, as well as by organic farmers themselves, because they know that the proposed law will make it difficult for them to operate organically. After all, it is a project full of legislative errors, chaos, created in an inaccessible and inconspicuous way. Minister! Today, Polish agriculture faces great challenges and faces many crises. The war on our border, earlier the COVID pandemic, has disrupted long, globalized supply chains of fertilisers, feed and food. The climate crisis brings droughts and storms to agriculture. Between 2010 and 2020, the number of farms decreased by around 130 000, which is close to 13%, mainly small and family farms. The answer to all these challenges and problems can be organic farming, organic farming run by family farms, with short supply chains, guaranteeing food security for Poles and Poles and healthy food. Organic farming needs its constitution today, it needs good law. Unfortunately, instead of the constitution of organic farming after 2 years of work, the Ministry of Agriculture presents a legal bubel, a project in which everything was put on its head, in which instead of keeping what is good and improving what is bad, it was saved what is bad and changed to worse what was good. It is a project in which editorial and legislative chaos prevails and which really requires a thorough improvement in dozens of places. This project was discussed at two meetings of the Committee on Agriculture with a large number of representatives and representatives of the social side, organic farmers, certification bodies. These two committee meetings were actually the moment when this project collided with the wall, failing to withstand the substantive criticism of the social side. High Izbo! Today's government bill will be a brake for the further development of organic farming in Poland, and perhaps even its nail to the coffin. Either we improve this project or reject it because it is simply impossible to accept it in its present form. As the Civic Coalition, we make eight amendments to eliminate the biggest barriers and errors of this project and to unlock the development of organic farming. The first important change is the broad discussion at the committee meeting on binding guidelines, which are nothing more than a non-constitutional duplication law, which will completely destabilise organic production in Poland, and you can simply use the possibility of issuing a regulation on this matter in accordance with the constitution and common practice. The second point is to remove the absurd provision on controlling organic farmers for 5 years from the date they withdraw from organic production. It is a recipe of even repressive nature for farmers who once had organic production and which discourages those who want to start producing it. Another point is to remove the need to pass an examination for an inspector of organic farming. Poland is the only country in the European Union where inspectors have to pass a ministerial examination, so the recruitment of inspectors by certification bodies is very blocked. High Izbo! Let us remove these absurdities from the bill, otherwise we will not meet the EU's objectives of the strategy from field to table and stop the development of organic farming for many years, and we will directly bring them down. Let us quietly adopt a law that is good for organic farming and give it a chance to develop. Let us give Polish farmers and farmers a chance to unleash their potential. Let us ensure food security, healthy and ecological food for Poles and Poles. [[♪ The bell ♪]] That should be our priority. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u111	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on a government draft law amending the Act - Environmental Law and some other laws. The project is included in print No 2181. The committee's report is contained in print No 2210. High Izbo! The proposal transposes into the Polish legal order two European Union Directives: Commission Delegated Directive 2021/1226 of 21 December 2020 amending for the purposes of adapting to scientific and technical progress Annex II to Directive 2002/49/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards common noise assessment methods and Commission Directive 2020/367 of 4 March 2020 amending Annex III to the abovementioned Directive of 2002/49/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the determination of methods for the assessment of environmental noise harmful effects. High Izbo! None of the Member States of the European Union has yet transposed both Directives, i.e. Directive 2021/1226 and Directive 367/2020. I can say that we are in the European vanguard in this matter. Directive 2020/367 imposes new obligations, while Directive 2021/1226 updates the noise assessment provisions and requires operators drawing up strategic noise maps to apply updated noise assessment methods taking into account the latest knowledge in this field. The new solutions introduced by Directive 2020/367 will require competent environmental authorities and bodies to draw up strategic noise maps to apply a uniform way of assessing the harmful effects of road, rail and air noise and to take corrective action to minimise these effects. In addition to the implementation of the two directives mentioned by me, the proposal amends national law, inter alia. amendments to the Act - Environmental law. Let me remind you that in the article. 237 Act - Environmental law is the obligation to draw up and submit an ecological review by road managers to the Marshal of the voivodship. Currently, the Marshal of the voivodship may impose this obligation on the managers of motorways and express roads in cities on the rights of the county. This is a limited scope. On the basis of such a review, an analysis of the environmental impact of the road shall be carried out and corrective action may be taken to reduce the negative action of the road. The present provisions of the project contained in print No 2181 extend the jurisdiction of the Marshal of the voivodship in this respect to other roads, which has an obvious effect. It's about protecting ourselves from road-related nuisance. Secondly, in the article. 73a Environmental law is a mandatory mandate for the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs to regulate by means of a regulation issued in agreement with the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and the Minister responsible for Construction, Spatial Planning and Housing, how to establish a safe distance at the location of new plants presenting a risk of major industrial accident and, secondly, new investments in the vicinity of such plants where major accidents may occur. This Regulation is also to specify the types of such accidents, impact limits and similar volumes. In any EU country, the issues I am talking about have not been fully addressed. It's about being mentioned in the article. 73a Environmental law The authorisation has become optional. It was nowhere to be said in such a categoric way. Thirdly, the draft law also amends the Act of 3 October 2008 on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments. Those amendments shall bring the provisions into order after the amendment introduced by the Act of 30 August 2019. It's about the numbering of the rules and other order, as you might say, legislative interference. They also delete unnecessary provisions concerning no longer established databases of environmental information, no longer functioning registers of hazardous substances and no longer functioning derogations in relation to acceptable levels of substances in the air. We update the provisions of the Act in accordance with current requirements. Fourthly, in the Act of 15 July 2011 on the National Eco-Management and Audit Scheme (EMAS) in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 there is a list of enforcement authorities whose opinion is to be taken into account in the EMAS registration procedure. Amendment to Article 3 of the project is supplemented by this list about the President of the National Water Farm of Poland, which conducts a number of procedures in the field of water law implementation, so it has reliable data on the impact of the applicant organisations on the aquatic environment. Let me remind you that the national eco-management and audit scheme for EMAS is simply a voluntary register to which companies who want to have a good opinion on how they respect the environment can sign up. Of course, if they meet the conditions. For this entry to be actually supported by reliable findings, the list of authorities that decide to complete this alert must be complete. We supplement it, as I say, with the President of the National Water Farm of Poland. This is, in short, the whole of the substantive changes that the project is making. Art. 4, 5 and 6 of the project contain transitional provisions and in accordance with Article. 7 The Act shall enter into force 14 days after its publication. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I kindly ask you to adopt a draft law amending the Act - Environmental Law and some other laws contained in the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry in print No 2210. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u113	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the government draft law amending the Act - Environmental Law and some other laws, print No 2181. This is a project aimed at implementing European Union law, which concerns the transposition of Directives 2021/1226 and 2020/367 of the European Union Commission, and amendments to environmental legislation against noise as regards common noise assessment methods and the establishment of methods for assessing environmental noise pollution. The Act amending the Act - Environmental Law aims to amend environmental regulations against noise in the form of more detailed rules on the characteristics of environmental authorities, namely the characteristics of the Marshal of the voivodship, a formal amendment to the regulations on the provision of environmental information and its protection and public participation in environmental protection, environmental impact assessments relating to strategic noise maps and environmental programmes against noise. In addition, the Act aims to give optional character to an implementing act by the Minister responsible for Climate Affairs in agreement with the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and the Minister responsible for Construction, Planning and Land Planning and Housing on: how to set a safe distance in the case of the location of new plants presenting a serious industrial accident and new investments in the vicinity of plants presenting a risk of major industrial accidents, types of major industrial accidents and parameters of the impact of potential damage, consequences of major industrial accidents. In addition: repeal of the provision concerning the provision of information on emissions and abstraction of water collected in the voivodship database on the use of the environment, repeal of the provision on the obligation to include in the Public Information Bulletin a decision on deferral and exclusion from the application of certain levels of permitted substances into the air, as well as the addition of a point which extends the list of law enforcement authorities, to the President of the State Water Farm of Poland. The solutions contained in the project aim to ensure the protection of the environment and human health against noise through the common noise assessment methods for all Member States of the European Union, the revised environmental noise assessment methods used in the preparation of strategic noise maps and the common methods for the assessment of environmental noise harmful effects applied to all Member States of the European Union in the framework of the drawing up of strategic noise maps, on the basis of which the environmental protection programme against noise is subsequently drawn up, as well as the equipment of the voivodship marshal with tools that enable users of the environment to be required to draw up and submit to the marshal environmental reviews as a basis for the determination of corrective obligations. This will allow for an in-depth and appropriate effort to identify the types of major industrial accidents and the location of establishments presenting a risk of major industrial accidents in relation to housing, public utility facilities, long-term crops, national roads and railway lines. The overall changes proposed in the draft law should have a positive impact on the environment, in particular on the sound environment and therefore on human health and well-being. The objective of the project cannot be achieved by other means, and it is therefore necessary to amend this law in line with the proposal made in the proposed regulation. Dear Marshal! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice are asking for further work on the proposed solutions. At the same time, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I make two amendments to the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u115	Dear Marshal! High Seym! We are dealing with the introduction into the Polish legal order of European law, the applicable directives which order, regulate, improve the quality of the fight against noise. In European legislation, we have been dealing with this type of activity for many years, which introduces different types of standards, harmonising different approaches at European level to the theme of comfort of life. I think that this directive is useful because it has already been among EU officials a dozen years ago, among the Members of the European Parliament there has been a growing awareness that excessive regulation is taking place, that too many different types of specific rights are being applied and introduced into legislation. Then a special study committee was set up, which should be removed from European legislation and how to shape European legislation in the future, so that it is not accused of being too detailed that it interferes too much with national legislation. Here we have an example of what is needed and necessary, because clean water and clean air are needed for living, for comfort of life. With regard to both clean water and clean air, awareness of the citizens of individual European Member States is clear and precise. Especially in Poland for several years now This is increasing in terms of fighting for clean air, financing and moving away from harmful energy substances, burning substances. And in the case of this consciousness, there was a quality leap. When it comes to noise, I think we're at the beginning of this road. That is why I am addressing the Minister in such a case, so as not to stop just implementing this directive and not be content with making it possible to analyse the fight against noise in more detail. Since these competences are shared between central and local authorities, training and conferences should simply be carried out to make local authorities, including central officials aware of the importance of activities in this area. I will give just one example in connection with this change of law, quote: As a result of this change, the content of the descriptive part and the strategic noise map is extended to assess the harmful effects to be drawn up in accordance with this Directive transposed into the Polish legal order. The purpose of this assessment is to determine the harmful effects of noise on humans, in particular the incidence of ischaemic heart disease and the occurrence of significant nuisance and significant sleep disturbances. This change is intended to inspire actions to minimise these negative impacts. In many European capitals, the issue of combating noise has taken the form of important actions of individual political parties, individual administrations in metropolitan areas, for several years. E.g. in Paris for several years now there is an office of Paris Merity, to which any citizen can report, protesting due to excessive noise in its surroundings or pointing to this noise. First, appropriate analytical action is being taken to assess the intensity of this noise. Then, possibly, changes are proposed to put an end to the matter. Few months ago, a year ago, special radars were introduced in Paris to measure noise. They're only being piloted for two years. What is the purpose of their introduction? They are to function as if they were speeding, i.e. when noise standards are exceeded, mandates will be issued. As I said, this is a pilot project that will be evaluated after 2 years. Speaking of which, I wanted to show you how important this issue is and that it should not be minimized. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u177	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Legal tasks of the cash register agricultural insurance repeatedly oblige that authority to request information from the central database of perpetual books. Taking into account the scale of the institution's activities and the fact that each individual is conducting cases, this gives a lot of time and work, which is devoted to a tedious process of individual examination of each application. The process of obtaining consent for multiple, unlimited search of perpetual books in a central database that would be legally properly secured provides a guarantee of processing this information without endangering the data contained. The changes proposed in this Act will also directly affect the work of the Central Information Books of Perpetuals themselves who are obliged to deal with individual applications. This will save time both on the existing side and in the body itself. This will also save you the difficulties of conducting correspondence or handling. In addition to these benefits to the Treasury, which are revealed in a faster and more effective recovery of due property claims, these changes will also benefit KRUS stakeholders, i.e. farmers, as data from the central register are used to calculate the contribution due for pension insurance, which ultimately translates into the amount of the pension benefit. The accelerated path of the procedure will reduce waiting time and the risk of confusion directly affecting the material situation of the citizen, which we also particularly care about. The amended amendment also provides for the definition of a catalogue of property components related to the proceedings, a criminal offence that involves the processing of criminal information. The current regulation was flawed in terms of creating a legal gap for the lack of collection of data on objects and immovable property obtained as a result of the crime, repeatedly liquefying extortionable assets, thus enabling de facto their loss through the application of a criminal measure in accordance with Article. 44 criminal code. It is therefore fully understandable to give the head of the National Criminal Information Centre the right to apply for permission for multiple and unlimited searches of perpetual books in the central database of perpetual books, which will also allow for much faster and more effective work that translates into the inevitability and severity of the measures taken against criminals. Of course, these arguments presented by me result in the support of the Law and Justice Club for the contested law. Thank you very much to the House and to your marshal.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u179	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It has to be said that these will be further institutions entitled to carry out such checks, and they will be almost 30 in total. Introduction changes require legislative changes to the law on the processing of criminal information, namely changes concerning the definition of the scope of the criminal information processed, so that this scope slightly extends to property related to criminals and crimes committed by them, so that the scope of the criminal information processed includes not only objects, including property, used to commit a crime or lost in connection with a crime, but also objects, including property, obtained as a result of a crime, i.e. objects, including property in which certain persons have been subject to criminal offences. The Civic Coalition Club supports the changes contained in this law, although we are somewhat concerned and surprised at the fact that they concern a rather minor amendment, which is of course important from the point of view of the conduct of the perpetual accounting procedure. In total, there are about 25 000 cases conducted by these two institutions. within a year, so to some extent it will speed up the conduct and entries in the everlasting books. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u180	Thank you very much, sir. Mrs Katarzyna Ueberhan, Club Left. Please state your position.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u181	Thank you. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Left Club, I am pleased to present my position on the Government's draft amendment to the perpetual books and mortgage law and the law on the processing of criminal information contained in print No 2203. According to the proposed regulations of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, KRUS, and the head of the National Criminal Information Centre will be given the opportunity to request permission from the Minister of Justice for multiple, unlimited search of perpetual books in the central database of perpetual books. The permission to search for perpetual books provided for in the Act is needed in order for these institutions to find the number of the perpetual book concerning property belonging to a particular person, by doing this search on the basis of criteria known to KRUS or the head of KCIK - e.g. the address of the property, the name, the name of the person concerned - and also to be able to check whether the person is the owner of any real estate in Poland, and if so, which particular property. The aim of the proposed regulation is above all to make it easier for these institutions to carry out their statutory tasks and to have a positive impact on the time these bodies deal with matters. Currently, the KRUS is required to claim unpaid insurance contributions and one of these forms is to provide insurance for farmers' social security contributions and health insurance through the entry of a compulsory mortgage in the debtor's perpetual property book. Thanks to the proposed solutions of the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund and the head of the National Criminal Information Centre, they will be able to request permission from the Minister for multiple and unlimited search of perpetual books in the central database. In order to obtain this access, they will have to comply with the conditions set out in the Everlasting Books and mortgage Act. This applies in particular to the conditions for the possession of electronic devices or systems enabling the person receiving the data from the central database of the perpetual books, the scope of the data and the date on which it is obtained. It is also about having technical and organisational safeguards to protect against the use of the data obtained which are not in accordance with the objective. The estimated number of requests sent by the KRUS to the central database of perpetual accounts for the provision of information is around 2700 applications per year. As regards the arrangements for criminal information related to objects, including property derived from crime, the scope of the criminal information processed will cover not only objects, including property, used to commit a crime or lost in connection with a crime, but also objects, including real estate, resulting from a crime. In such a situation, criminal information would cover inter alia. data on real estate that have been acquired through specific persons as a result of e.g. money laundering. As regards the proposed solutions, as a result of their adoption more actors will have access to the central database of perpetual books. And although new solutions will only enter into force 2 years after the publication in the Official Journal - I assume that it is a matter of time needed to adapt the ICT system and infrastructure - but the change is necessary, on purpose, as it will facilitate the performance of their statutory tasks to institutions, it will reduce the time taken by these units. Therefore, the Left Club supports this submission, of course also bearing in mind other proceedings in the perpetual books and looking forward to the government presentation, which will also improve other proceedings in the perpetual books section. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]]

We proceed with the examination of item 5 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Act on personal income tax and certain other laws (Prints No. 2186 and 2239). I would like to ask Mrs Gabriela Masłowska to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u213	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Sejm at its 53rd meeting on 27 April in accordance with Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm have been sent by the Government to the Committee on Public Finance to review the Act on personal income tax and certain other laws. After considering the above draft law at its meeting on 10 May 2022, the Public Finance Commission asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. The draft law submitted on the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act referred to in the print report 2239 aims at simplifying and improving the tax system currently in force. For many years, tax laws have raised concerns, among others. on the issue of instability, high fiscalism and the sense of injustice due to the non-social role of tax redistribution, which was particularly highlighted by the low- and medium-sized groups. Aware of the challenges in this area, the Law and Justice Government has attempted to amend its tax laws to address problems, including problems. within the framework of the Polish Order. However, the scope, depth and pace of the changes have highlighted problems in the practical application of the new rules, such as the ambiguities of certain provisions, inconsistencies and complexity. By monitoring these problems, the government has taken a swift and - it must be acknowledged - a courageous decision to correct errors in cooperation with dozens of actors and organisations, which results in the project being processed today. The main effect of the reform of the tax system, as well as the current project, is among others. a reduction in the tax wedge for virtually all taxpayers, including those earning above average wages. Here the most important solutions are a reduction in the tax rate in the first tax scale from 17% to 12%. and restore the possibility of deduction of health insurance premiums for persons engaged in business and clearing of linear tax, lump sum and tax card. These are positive changes because the tax-free amount will increase to 30,000. PLN as well as income threshold on a scale of up to 120 thousand. zł, as it turned out in practice, did not compensate for the consequences of not being able to deduct social security contributions. Another important solution in this project is to restore preferential taxation of the income of single parents while excluding from the taxable income of the parent of the pension received by children, including the survivor's pension, and to increase the annual income earned by the child up to 25 years old from PLN 3089 to around 15 thousand. zł without the risk of a parent's loss of preferences. PIT-0 is also extended to maternity benefits. The possibility to deduct expenditure on the purchase of historic real estate is also removed, leaving the possibility to deduct expenditure on conservation or renovation works of historic real estate, but only those which are in the register of monuments, because this issue of counting expenditure on the purchase of historic real estate raised many social concerns. Another solution is the introduction of a supplementary mechanism due to a decrease in the amount of 1% write-downs. tax on public benefit organisations due to a reduction in the overall tax revenue pool. The tax absolution introduced since 2022 is also abolished where flat-rate taxable income has not been fully or even partially declared by the taxpayer. This kind of absolution is endured. Around these problems, because these are the most important issues to which this project is concerned, there has been a discussion. Many amendments have been tabled. Most of the amendments were editorial and legislative. Of course, we have managed to solve these problems with the help of the Legal Office of the Sejm, for which we thank the employees of the Legislative Office working with the Public Finance Commission - for this enormous effort, speed and competence. Without your help, it would be much harder for us to deal with these problems in such a short time. Substantive amendments were also tabled, inter alia. in this matter, which I was talking about, in order to relieve the monuments only to cover conservation work. The next amendments concerned an attempt to clarify the way in which taxpayers withdraw and amend their statements and requests, to extend the deadline for entering contract data in the register and to increase their value to PLN 1500 without VAT until 1 January 2023, among others. for local government units, as well as the exemption from the revenue of funds transferred by PFRON from 10 March 2022, as the relevant provisions entered into force on that date. There were also amendments and proposals to abolish the savings tax, so-called. Belka's tax. There were also proposals by Members to change the mechanism for calculating compensation for NGOs in relation to the proposed project, as well as to change the way in which single parents are billed against the tax. During the discussion, the Legislative Office reported, and some Members reported a problem such as that the change of tax laws takes place during the tax year and that they are not always beneficial and often unfavourable changes, such as. elimination of reliefs. Specifically addressing the issue of the elimination of reliefs: it is known that, like any liquidation of the reliefs, this is a negative change. It has been stressed that such a procedure violates the fundamental principles of the legal security of citizens' confidence in the rule of law. At the same time, it was stressed that some of the provisions of the Act, or rather the amendments tabled to this project do not concern income tax, but concern completely different contracts, relate to other laws, such as the Penal Code, the Public Finance Act... It's about the Penal Code, it's about the provisions of the article. 10, therefore, go beyond the subject-matter of the project, beyond the subject-matter of the Act and have therefore been introduced without first reading. Following this discussion, a vote took place, where 24 members of the finance committee voted in favour, 14 abstained, no one opposed it. The Committee on Public Finance therefore proposes that the attached draft be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The discussion that took place during the public consultation, where we listened to all parties, also concerned such preferences for families, but this preference remained solely for the lonely, and this preference gives an average benefit of PLN 1525 per year for those single parents. As a result, more than 0.5 million single parents will pay less tax. I understand, of course, that with regard to the original project that we have proposed, there is a difference between a child with a disability, when it is an amount of 30,000. the amount free, and the other children, when it is half of this amount, while, to be clear - here I am addressing Mrs Wielichowska, because she mentioned widowers - in this matter we have two and a half times, because the family pension in this project is treated as a separate income, with a separate amount free. This discussion has led us to have a mechanism that will be enshrined in the regulation. The Regulation will set out criteria and these measures will be allocated in part proportional to the proposals made by taxpayers and will be distributed in part in competition with the Council for Public Use, i.e. the identification of NGOs themselves, because in this Council the majority are representatives of the organisation, so as not to exclude these small organisations as well. Why 1.5% Sounds good, and it's not a solution? Because 1.5% from zero when it comes to PIT, that's exactly 1% from zero. In this sense, that obviously doesn't solve this issue. This solution we have proposed is a better solution. There are no three tax systems in a year. During the entire tax year, from 1 January to 31 December, there will be a tax system which we will now introduce - in advance from 1 July - in an annual statement from 1 January to 31 December. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u321	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Public Finance on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services, print No. 2182. The Sejm at its 53rd meeting on 28 April 2022, as provided for in Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Public Finances for its consideration. The draft law amending the law on value added tax provides for amendments to the Act of 11 March 2004 on value added tax for goods and services aimed at implementing Council Directive (EU) 2019/2235 of 16 December 2019 amending the Directive on the common system of value added tax and Directive 2008/118 on general rules on excise duty for defence activities within the Union. The directive, which I mention at the beginning, is to be implemented in the national legal order by 30 June 2022. It should be noted here that this draft law does not implement the article. Point 1 (3) and Article 1. 2 of the Amending Directive - not to go into detail at the moment - into the value added tax system, hereinafter referred to as the VAT Directive, and the excise duty law. To a wider extent, this is described in the explanatory memorandum of this draft law. As far as the implementation of the directive I am talking about is concerned, Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) c The VAT Directive provides, under certain conditions, for exemption from value added tax for goods supplied and services provided to the armed forces of each State party to the North Atlantic Treaty and for goods imported by those forces when they participate in joint defence activities outside their own State. Such exemptions so far have not included actions carried out by the armed forces of a Member State taking part in EU projects under the Common Security and Defence Policy. In its Joint Communication of 28 March 2018 on the Action Plan for Military Mobility, the High Representative of the Union for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy and the Commission recognised the overall need to harmonise VAT applicable to defence activities within the European Union. The aim of the Directive is therefore to harmonise VAT taxation on defence activities within the European Union and NATO. The amendments that are modelled on the existing VAT rules include: firstly, the exemption from VAT for the supply of goods and services in the European Union intended for the use of the armed forces of a Member State or the accompanying civilian staff or for the supply of their messes or canteens; secondly, the exemption from VAT for the importation of goods into Member States by the armed forces of other Member States for the use of those forces or the accompanying civilian personnel or for the supply of their messes or canteens; thirdly, the recognition as an intra-Community acquisition of goods by the armed forces of a Member State participating in defence operations carried out in the framework of the Union's CSDP activities for the use of those forces or their accompanying civil servants. During the committee's work, three amendments were tabled, which were welcomed by the committee. The Public Finance Commission after considering the above draft law at its meeting on 10 May this year. requests its adoption, the adoption of a bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u323	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice expresses a positive opinion on the Government bill amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services, contained in print No 2182. The report is currently in print 2226. The rapporteur just said what is the essence of this project. He also mentioned that at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, three amendments were tabled during the examination of the project, which were accepted. Of course, Law and Justice also vote in favour of these amendments. Taking advantage of the fact that I can speak, I would like to draw attention briefly to what these amendments relate to. This could be an important issue for stakeholders. The aim of the amendment, which was to amend the Act on the control of certain investments and the Act on interest subsidies on bank loans granted to traders affected by the effects of COVID-19, is to protect Polish companies considered important for order and security or public health against hostile acquisitions. It was assumed that the provisions, which were to apply for 24 months from the date of entry into force, i.e. only until 25 July 2022, would apply for 60 months. The period of validity of these provisions has been extended. This is due to the fact that the pandemic problems have lasted longer than initially assumed and that there have also been additional circumstances in recent months which may be accompanied by distortions on the domestic and Union markets for competition. This includes, for example, sanctions imposed on Russia and Belarus in connection with the war in Ukraine, which can result not only in rising fuel prices, energy prices, production costs for entrepreneurs, but also in increasing the prices of raw materials and semi-finished products used by entrepreneurs. Costs for subcontractors may also increase. All of this can cause some companies to face some liquidity problems. This may encourage the acquisition of these companies by other investors. In order to safeguard this, we accept an amendment which extends the duration of these provisions. This means that certain entities wishing to acquire or gain significant participation or dominance in another company will still be required to notify the President of the OOKiK. In the event of infringements by these entities, penalties may continue to be imposed on them. That's one of those corrections. The second amendment concerns the question of extending the time limits for reduced excise duties. Well, ladies and gentlemen, the project provides for an extension of the temporary reduction in excise duty rates on electricity, certain motor fuels, diesel, biocomponents constituting self-fuels, motor gasoline and LPG and exemption from excise duty on electricity used by households and temporary exemption from retail tax on fuels. The proposed amendments to the excise duty law will prolong the validity of the reduced rates of excise duty on the abovementioned. energy products up to a minimum excise duty, including fuel and emission charges, in such a way that they will remain at their current level by 31 July 2022. So we have good news. The reduction of excise duty on motor fuels, fuel oil and energy and exemption from excise duty on electricity for households will be the sole cost of the state budget estimated at PLN 541 million including VAT. The cost of extending the tax exemption for the State budget for retail tax is estimated at about PLN 55 million. This is the second amendment. We still have a third amendment, which we also accept. It concerns exports of defence equipment and related provisions in view of the situation in Ukraine. The simplified procedures (The Bell) which were introduced in relation to exports of protective equipment to Ukraine were introduced for a period of 2 months - from 17 March to 17 May. With the provisions of this Law and the amendment which Law and Justice also supports, they will remain in force until the end of 2022. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u329	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, 2 weeks ago, we started working on a draft amendment to the law on taxation on goods and services. 2 weeks ago, it was a clear, clear legal text aimed at implementing into the Polish legal order the provisions of the European Union directive ordering VAT to be harmonised for defence activities within the European Union and NATO. In particular, the arrangements contained therein introduced a VAT exemption for the supply of goods and services in the European Union for the use of the armed forces of a Member State or the accompanying civilian staff or for the supply of their messes or canteens and the exemption from VAT for the importation of goods into the Member States by the armed forces of other Member States for the use of those forces or the accompanying civilian personnel or for the supply of their messes or canteens. These exemptions, it is worth noting, can only apply to situations where the armed forces carry out tasks directly related to defence activities within the framework of the common security and defence policy of the European Union, and must not include civilian missions carried out within the framework of that policy. Goods or services supplied for the purpose of civil staff could therefore only be exempted if civilian personnel are accompanied by armed forces carrying out tasks directly related to defence activities under the common security and defence policy outside their Member State. It is worth mentioning that the Union's defence activities under the common policy include military missions and operations, the activities of combat groups, mutual assistance, ongoing structural cooperation projects and the activities of the European Defence Agency. The European Commission itself concluded that, under the current circumstances, there is a significantly increased risk that foreign direct investment by Russian and Belarusian investors could pose a threat to security and public order. Therefore, the proposed amendment does not change the scope of the current Act on the control of certain investments. First of all, I wanted to thank the initiators of this short bill, because for a good cause, it's just two articles. The subject must be well known to all of you. Especially during the pandemic period, it turned out that the pharmacists, but not only them, because also pharmaceutical technicians who work in pharmacies, met with various acts of aggression directed towards them and hence the request from the pharmaceutical community to extend protection, which is already in other medical professions, to this professional group. The parliamentary initiative, then appropriate, needed amendments in committee, which were adopted by all, which clarified this initiative. Of course, the amendments adopted in cooperation with the government. What is it? We have made adjustments so that, first of all, it is clear that it is about the work of the pharmacist and about protection in relation to the work done, so that there is no doubt that only in this respect is protection allocated. However, as with other medical professions, it is important that there is no distinction that would give greater protection than other medical professions. And the other thing. As I mentioned at the beginning, and this also required a change in the title of the law, most often or in very many cases. it is not only pharmacists, but also pharmaceutical technicians who sell certain products in pharmacies and also provide advice. In this respect, the aim was also to provide this professional group with adequate safeguards and appropriate support. This is where an additional article appeared in the Act concerning this professional group. In this case, it was necessary to refer not to the pharmaceutical profession law, but to the pharmaceutical law, so that a group of professional pharmaceutical technicians could also be covered by these provisions. As I said, I think there was a full understanding - I can say that on behalf of the committee - and there was full agreement on the need for adopted regulation, which was also adopted with great satisfaction by the entire pharmaceutical community. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u571	I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft law on port reception facilities for ship waste in view of the need to transpose Directive 2019/883 of the European Parliament and of the Council on port reception facilities for ship waste into the Polish legal order, print No 2223. The proposed regulations transpose into the Polish legal order the provisions of the EU Directive 2019/883 on port reception facilities from ships. The new law makes it necessary to repeal the existing Act of 12 September 2002 on port reception facilities and cargo residues from ships. The Act will apply to any vessel using seaports or seaports, excluding inter alia. ships engaged in public service. Fishing, historic and recreational vessels of less than 45 m length will be excluded from prior notification of waste. Compared to the existing law, the essential elements of the proposed legislation are, among other things, and here are a few: to regulate all types of waste generated on ships - so far they have not included e.g. waste related to air pollution and harmful liquid substances; clarification of the conditions to be met by port reception facilities from ships with regard to the volume of traffic in port and the possibility of selective reception of waste; clarification of the scope of the ship's master's obligation to inform port of the waste on board using the new national single point of contact system. Ports and seaports will continue to be required to develop ship waste management plans. On the other hand, the re-approval period for these plans has been extended from 3 to 5 years. This is beneficial for port managers or maritime marinas from both a financial and an administrative point of view. Ports and seaports will be required to issue appropriate receipts to shipmasters regarding the content of the waste. Seaports which only have self-service reception facilities will be exempted from the obligation to issue receipts. The new rules also regulate the fees paid by shipowners for the disposal and treatment of ship-source waste. An intermediate fee will be introduced, which will have to be paid each time ships are wrapped up in ports and marinas, regardless of the actual discharge of waste into reception facilities, which will discourage illegal discharges of waste into the sea in accordance with the principle that the charge in port will still be incurred. Ships will also have the right to pass passively caught waste without additional charges. This is an important solution for the maritime fisheries sector. The proposed rules improve ship waste management systems. The ban on waste discharges in the Baltic Sea area is one of the cornerstones of regulation adopted at both European Union and international level, i.e. International Maritime Organisation, IMO. In order to implement this approach, the Directive requires Member States to implement appropriate infrastructure and systems solutions, which are reflected in the proposed law. The Marshal of the Sejm, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure, sent the above draft law to the committee on 9 May 2022 in order to carry out a first reading. On 11 May the first reading of the Maritime Affairs Committee was held. During the committee's work, Members tabled four clarification amendments that were adopted. The remaining amendments of the editorial nature tabled by the Legislative Bureau were also unanimously adopted by the committee. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission has adopted the draft law unanimously and therefore recommends that the High House adopt this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u573	Dear Marshal, Minister! The Baltic Sea Directives have not always been effective, as we know, but in this case we have no doubt that this is the right idea. 1/3 of the pollutants that come out into our sea come from shipping activities, 2/3 come from land activities, and this tightening of the screw is needed to ensure that our water, the Baltic, can also function economically, so that, for example, fishing can function in the Baltic Sea, that the sea is economically, touristly and industrially attractive. That is why these regulations need to be implemented, and pollution must be made less and less into the Baltic Sea. At the same time, this directive explicitly introduces a collection charge to the extent that the ports determine this. It is very good that these costs will not be borne by our port companies, but will only be borne by shipowners, who also through these charges will have to offset the costs associated with adapting the port infrastructure, which will have to be extended due to these new obligations, but as you know perfectly well, maritime transport has been experiencing a real hoss for several years. As far as this branch of the economy is concerned, the profits of the shipowners have been really colossal lately, so I am convinced that the shipowners will be perfectly able to handle it. So, yes, we need to support it and put it into practice. I will end with what we discussed at the Maritime Affairs Committee meeting. The subject is a bit side-by-side, but is generally related to environmental protection and the implementation of this directive. This is Russia's attitude, which is our partner in the use of the Baltic Sea, which we, the countries of the European Union, are trying very hard to take care of. As you know, those who are interested in the Baltic, Russia is at the other end when it comes to the ecological approach to these issues, and in fact, even though it has limited access to the Baltic, its coastline is not that long, it does not fully respect the different standards that we take upon ourselves. Therefore, even though the sanctions currently imposed seriously hinder Russians shipping in the Baltic Sea and docking our ports, we need to have the back of our heads when Ukraine wins the beastly attitude of the Russians in this war, defeats Moscow - and we need to remember that - before we bring the Russians back to our ports, we will have to start thinking about the requirement that they respect the environmental element of the Baltic Sea basin. There will be a good opportunity for this now or in a few months, and if the situation requires it, then also in a few years, when again it will be possible to talk civilizedly with Russia in some way. So, yes, we support this EU initiative, we think it is right. In fact, the Government of Law and Justice has supported this initiative from the outset. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u575	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the Civic Coalition on the Act on Port Equipment for Waste Receiving from Ships, print No 2223. This law is amended by the September 2006 Act. Only seemingly can the bill seem technical and invalid. Besides, the High House is not involved in the adoption of invalid laws, but at the very least is dealing with the bad ones or the good ones. The bill is very important because it concerns the protection of the environment and, in particular, the protection of marine assets against pollution and waste from ships. This Regulation implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 on port reception facilities for ship waste. The government proposal for a law on port reception facilities from ships is late. We should have passed this bill a year earlier, but better late than never. It's not that big of a deal. Research has shown that as much as 30% of waste is generated by marine sources in the waters of the European Union. In addition, 12 million tons of plastic waste are estimated to be produced on ships each year and goes to seas and oceans. These data speak to us and impose on our generation an obligation to take all available measures which will radically reduce - it would be best if they eliminated - this is dramatic littering of waters. In describing the more accessible language of the proposal and the objectives of the bill, I would say that it is a matter for Member States and other signatories to the implementing rules to adapt the technical conditions to ensure that in ports different waste generated on ships can be collected in an environmentally sound manner. The ship's ban on dumping waste into the sea alone is not enough. In ports of Member States, technical and logistical conditions must be created for the collection of all types of waste from ships. The Act on port reception facilities for ship waste sets out the obligations and responsibilities of all operators for the implementation of the provisions of this Act, including in particular: the obligations of port managers to ensure access to port reception facilities for ship waste, the rules for drawing up ship waste management plans, the rules for setting fees for the maintenance and operation of port reception facilities for ship waste and the rules for supervision by maritime administrations of compliance with the provisions of the draft Act. The Act regulates the obligations and responsibilities of all entities in a clear and consistent manner with European Union law. The Civic Coalition took an active and active part in the work of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on this law. The Civic Coalition supports the adoption of a law on port reception facilities from ships and asks the High House to adopt this law. Thank you, Minister. Thank you, Marshal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u611	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The case concerns an urgent government draft law amending the law on assistance to Ukrainian citizens in connection with armed conflict in the territory of that State and the Act on informationization of activities of entities carrying out public tasks. This bill was discussed in our committee, the first reading of the bill took place on 10 May. It was presented by the Minister. There has been a discussion and this draft law has been considered, making some adjustments, some changes, some clarifications that have made this project even better, is good, will actually serve and will solve the underlying problem. Because what is this bill and what problem does it solve? This is primarily a matter of work, of employment. We want to support those who come to us from Ukraine, refugees from Ukraine who are looking for work here. The Act states in its records that the Minister responsible for Information Technology will ensure the functioning of the relevant IT system. Yes, the Minister of Computerization. Someone might ask: Why was this law not considered, and why did the first reading not take place in the Commission for Digitization, but only in the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs? I would like to point out that it is our committee, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, which examines numerous draft laws relating to assistance to Ukrainian citizens, which examines numerous legal solutions in support of citizens, in the field of the general, broadly understood problems arising from conflict in this part of the world, caused by aggressive assault, invasion, barbaric invasion of Russia into Ukraine. It is in our committee that such projects are being considered. So the Marshal of the Sejm decided to send it to our committee. To put it immodestly, it is our committee, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, that we could say boldly, that is the leader in dealing with such projects. Thus, this project has been considered and it is precisely foreseen that the Minister responsible for IT will ensure the functioning of a special IT system. It will be a kind of platform where workers who want to work, citizens of Ukraine and employers will meet. About 500,000 are expected. people, Ukrainian citizens will probably be able to benefit from this system by responding to suggestions or job offers from over 60 000. employers. What I am quoting, of course, are numbers that will probably be difficult to verify. Some estimates need to be taken and this is what has been proposed. Users of the ICT system, i.e. people who want to look for work through this system, will have to meet one basic condition. The citizens of Ukraine will have to have a PESEL number, which is given in accordance with Article. 4. So this is the condition to be able to create a profile in this system and to find the dream or work that really meets their expectations with this system. Another issue that was discussed at the meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, and we pointed out very clearly that the creation of a profile, the use of this ICT system is voluntary. Perhaps it is obvious to many of us, it is the intention of the government, but also as parliamentarians, as members of the committee, we agreed that this should be a voluntary provision, that if the Ukrainian citizen wants to find a job in this way, he wants to make it easier for him to find a good job, he will be able to benefit from it. Of course, the Ukrainian citizen will be able to update the data that will be entered there and will also have the right to delete this data. The security of these personal data will, of course, be protected by the Minister for Information Technology and he will have a duty, e.g. If the user deletes its profile, the system will, of course, have to delete all data from the whole system immediately. In this system will be included, among others. data such as name, date of birth, PESEL number, age, sex, nationality, e-mail address, telephone number, education, professional qualifications and the course of employment. This is undoubtedly another bill that really supports Ukrainian citizens, which supports all Ukraine, given what is happening in its territory, that is, in this great fight against the war criminal, against Putin. Their heroism - indeed we observe that Ukraine has been defending bravely for more than 70 days. It is our duty, the duty of parliamentarians, the duty of the High House, to support the Ukrainians, to support their citizens, to support Ukraine during this difficult period. I would like to thank all those who are involved in this assistance very warmly. This is not only a government that supports these actions very strongly, which prepares the relevant legal provisions, which propose such legislative proposals, but also local authorities, are primarily small municipalities. Municipalities, small units of local government, but also counties, cities on the rights of powiats, are also local governments of voivodships. Everyone is very much involved in this help. They are also NGOs that have a huge impact on the support of refugees who come to us from Ukraine and want to find shelter here. It is above all the support, as I said, of NGOs, that the support of Polish citizens, who have opened their hearts, opened their homes and supported refugees in this way, supports Ukraine in this way. And this is very important, and I would like to thank you very warmly for that and once again to stress that this law is the one that goes against it. This law is another idea which aims to support Ukrainian citizens so that they can actually be helped to find work, because it was during individual meetings of the government party, the local government party, non-governmental organisations that were emphasized that it would be useful, it would be worth to create an information and communication system that would support Ukrainian citizens in their search for work. Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I submit a draft law from print No 2240 and ask the High House to adopt and adopt this draft law. The access to data covering age, gender, education, professional qualifications and the course of employment will be given to those who post job offers, and thus to employers themselves, to those providing services related to the provision of job offers or to those providing placement services between employers and jobseekers. ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u671	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! 20 June 2022 will be 40 years after the death of the exceptional Pole, Zygmunt Piotr Bohusz-Szyszko. I want to remind him of his character. He was born on 19 January 1893 in Chełm as the son of Major of the Tsarist armies Jakub Krzysztof Szyszko and Helena of Witowski, a student of Ignacy Paderewski. Raised in the spirit of patriotic independence, he received a thorough education. He started his studies at the National Secondary School in Chelm. He continued his education in the Cadet Corps in Psków and Aleksandrów Podchorażych School in Moscow. During World War I as leader of the platoon, then the company in 12. The Astrachan Grenadier regiment fought on the Austrian front. He was badly wounded in captivity and was interned in Hungary, where he was in POW camps until 1918. After the end of World War I he joined the ranks of the reborn Polish Army. In October 1918, he was promoted to lieutenant. He took a training course in Dęblin. During the Polish-Bolshevik war he fought on the northern front as commander of the company of heavy machine guns took part in all fights of this regiment. During Płock's defense, he was badly wounded. For showing extraordinary courage and commanding skills in defence of Płock, Marshal Józef Piłsudski awarded him with the Silver Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari. In the autumn of 1921 he was sent to the Warsaw Higher War School, which he completed with very good results. In 1932, he was appointed Deputy Commander of the Border Protection Corps, 2 years later as Commander 1. The Legion Infantry Division in Vilnius. In Paris, chief general Władysław Sikorski appointed him commander. 1. The Grenadier Division and then entrusted him with organizing and commanding the Independent Podhale Rifle Brigade. In April 1940, he was promoted to Brigadier General. Next thing you know, he's in charge of nearly $5,000. The soldiers sailed to Norway, where, in command of the Podhale Rifle Brigade, he took part in the battle of Narwik. The German troops that fought in this area were almost completely destroyed, but due to the deteriorating situation in France, the commander-in-chief interrupted the operation of Polish troops in Norway. The Podhale Brigade was directed to battles in Brittany and soon dissolved. In October 1940, Władysław Sikorski awarded him the Golden Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari and the Norwegian War Cross with the Sword. In March 1942, he became Chief of Staff of the Polish Army in the Soviet Union. After the evacuation of the Polish army to Iraq, he was appointed commander of 5. The Ocean Infantry Division in the Middle East. He soon became Deputy Commander of the Second Corps of General Władysław Anders. He participated in all the battles of the Italian campaign, among others. Monte Cassino, Ankona and Bologna. In 1947, he left Italy for Great Britain, choosing to live in exile. While living there, he devoted himself to the work of the Combatant and the work of publishing and writing. He published articles and memories in London's weekly magazines. [[♪ The bell ♪]] He was an active member of the Polish Literature Association. He's had a lot of functions. He died on June 20, 1982, having lived for 89 years. According to the general's request, his ashes were brought to Poland. In 1997, the Council He was buried in Warsaw's Powązki with all the honors worthy of a hero. We remember our heroes. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-05-11-sejm-54-1.u675	On 18 May 2022, the 78th anniversary of the Battle of Monte will be celebrated. Cassino in Italy, one of the most important and fierce battles determining the fate of World War II. Monte Hill Cassino and the Benedictine monastery at its top were crucial for the German defence system on the so-called. Gustav's line. German units controlling the fortified monastery easily resisted attacks by allies, and subsequent attacks by American, British, French, Canadian, South African, New Zealand and Indian units ended in failure. On 18 May 1944, after extremely bloody fights lasting almost a week, troops of the 2nd Polish Corps under the command of General Władysław Anders won ruins of the Benedictine monastery on Monte Hill Cassino, Italy. The victory of the Polish weapons contributed to breaking the German line of defense and opening up the way for allies to liberate Rome. General Władysław Anders' soldiers did their job, but the approach to Monte's ruins Cassino was taken with heavy losses. In total, 923 soldiers were killed in the battle, 931 were wounded and 345 were found missing. The battle cost a total of about 70,000. soldiers of all the fighting nations. At the place of eternal rest the conquerors of Monte Cassino were carved with the inscription: A passerby, tell Poland that we have fallen faithful in her service. The inhabitants of Włoszczów County participated in the fights. At Monte Cassino they fought among others.: Józef Majecki and Czesław Stolarczyk from Oleszna, Stanisław Klim from Podleska, Eugeniusz Kotulski from Włoszczów, Wincenty Bąk from Rogienic, Stanisław Bawol from Bebelna, Czesław Kaczkowski, Kazimierz Młyńczak. This year, during the official celebrations of the 78th anniversary of the battle, the first march of the historic trail of the Battle of Monte will take place. Cassino, which will be implemented by the Military Publishing Institute and the Polish National Foundation. The 12-kilometer track is marked with 12 tables set in symbolic places connected with the fight of Poles over the hill. They feature short information in Polish, Italian and English, as well as pictures and situational maps. It is a form of telling the history of the participation of Poles in the battle and is intended to emphasize their importance.
